     1→/**
     2→ * Share Service - API integration for user-to-user sharing
     3→ *
     4→ * Wraps the generated Orval API client for share endpoints.
     5→ * All sharing operations flow through these functions.
     6→ *
     7→ * Security: The server never sees plaintext keys. All keys are
     8→ * ECIES-wrapped for the recipient before being sent to the API.
     9→ */
    10→
    11→import {
    12→  sharesControllerCreateShare,
    13→  sharesControllerGetReceivedShares,
    14→  sharesControllerGetSentShares,
    15→  sharesControllerLookupUser,
    16→  sharesControllerGetShareKeys,
    17→  sharesControllerAddShareKeys,
    18→  sharesControllerRevokeShare,
    19→  sharesControllerHideShare,
    20→  sharesControllerGetPendingRotations,
    21→  sharesControllerUpdateShareEncryptedKey,
    22→  sharesControllerCompleteRotation,
    23→} from '../api/shares/shares';
    24→
    25→import { wrapKey, bytesToHex, hexToBytes, generateRandomBytes } from '@cipherbox/crypto';
    26→import type { ReceivedShare, SentShare } from '../stores/share.store';
    27→import { useShareStore } from '../stores/share.store';
    28→import type { FolderNode } from '../stores/folder.store';
    29→
    30→/**
    31→ * Fetch all active, non-hidden shares received by the current user.
    32→ * Updates the share store with the results.
    33→ */
    34→export async function fetchReceivedShares(): Promise<ReceivedShare[]> {
    35→  const response = await sharesControllerGetReceivedShares();
    36→
    37→  return response.map((s) => ({
    38→    shareId: s.shareId,
    39→    sharerPublicKey: s.sharerPublicKey,
    40→    itemType: s.itemType as 'folder' | 'file',
    41→    ipnsName: s.ipnsName,
    42→    itemName: s.itemName,
    43→    encryptedKey: s.encryptedKey,
    44→    createdAt: String(s.createdAt),
    45→  }));
    46→}
    47→
    48→/**
    49→ * Fetch all active shares sent by the current user.
    50→ * Updates the share store with the results.
    51→ */
    52→export async function fetchSentShares(): Promise<SentShare[]> {
    53→  const response = await sharesControllerGetSentShares();
    54→
    55→  return response.map((s) => ({
    56→    shareId: s.shareId,
    57→    recipientPublicKey: s.recipientPublicKey,
    58→    itemType: s.itemType as 'folder' | 'file',
    59→    ipnsName: s.ipnsName,
    60→    itemName: s.itemName,
    61→    createdAt: String(s.createdAt),
    62→  }));
    63→}
    64→
    65→/**
    66→ * Check if a CipherBox user exists with the given secp256k1 public key.
    67→ * Returns false if no user is found (404).
    68→ *
    69→ * @param publicKeyHex - Uncompressed secp256k1 public key (0x04... format)
    70→ */
    71→export async function lookupUser(publicKeyHex: string): Promise<boolean> {
    72→  try {
    73→    await sharesControllerLookupUser({ publicKey: publicKeyHex });
    74→    return true;
    75→  } catch (err: unknown) {
    76→    // Only treat 404 as "user not found"; re-throw network/server errors
    77→    if (
    78→      err &&
    79→      typeof err === 'object' &&
    80→      'status' in err &&
    81→      (err as { status: number }).status === 404
    82→    ) {
    83→      return false;
    84→    }
    85→    throw err;
    86→  }
    87→}
    88→
    89→/**
    90→ * Create a new share, sharing an encrypted folder or file with another user.
    91→ *
    92→ * @param params.recipientPublicKey - Recipient's secp256k1 public key
    93→ * @param params.itemType - 'folder' or 'file'
    94→ * @param params.ipnsName - IPNS name of the shared item
    95→ * @param params.itemName - Display name of the shared item
    96→ * @param params.encryptedKey - Hex-encoded ECIES ciphertext of the item key
    97→ * @param params.childKeys - Optional re-wrapped descendant keys
    98→ */
    99→export async function createShare(params: {
   100→  recipientPublicKey: string;
   101→  itemType: 'folder' | 'file';
   102→  ipnsName: string;
   103→  itemName: string;
   104→  encryptedKey: string;
   105→  childKeys?: Array<{ keyType: 'file' | 'folder'; itemId: string; encryptedKey: string }>;
   106→}): Promise<{ shareId: string }> {
   107→  const response = await sharesControllerCreateShare({
   108→    recipientPublicKey: params.recipientPublicKey,
   109→    itemType: params.itemType,
   110→    ipnsName: params.ipnsName,
   111→    itemName: params.itemName,
   112→    encryptedKey: params.encryptedKey,
   113→    childKeys: params.childKeys?.map((k) => ({
   114→      keyType: k.keyType,
   115→      itemId: k.itemId,
   116→      encryptedKey: k.encryptedKey,
   117→    })),
   118→  });
   119→
   120→  return { shareId: response.shareId };
   121→}
   122→
   123→/**
   124→ * Revoke a share (soft-delete). Only the sharer can revoke.
   125→ * Keys are kept for lazy rotation.
   126→ */
   127→export async function revokeShare(shareId: string): Promise<void> {
   128→  await sharesControllerRevokeShare(shareId);
   129→}
   130→
   131→/**
   132→ * Hide a share from the recipient's view. Only the recipient can hide.
   133→ */
   134→export async function hideShare(shareId: string): Promise<void> {
   135→  await sharesControllerHideShare(shareId);
   136→}
   137→
   138→/**
   139→ * Get all re-wrapped child keys for a share.
   140→ * Accessible by both sharer and recipient.
   141→ */
   142→export async function fetchShareKeys(
   143→  shareId: string
   144→): Promise<Array<{ keyType: 'file' | 'folder'; itemId: string; encryptedKey: string }>> {
   145→  const response = await sharesControllerGetShareKeys(shareId);
   146→
   147→  return response.map((k) => ({
   148→    keyType: k.keyType as 'file' | 'folder',
   149→    itemId: k.itemId,
   150→    encryptedKey: k.encryptedKey,
   151→  }));
   152→}
   153→
   154→/**
   155→ * Add re-wrapped child keys to an existing share. Only the sharer can add keys.
   156→ */
   157→export async function addShareKeys(
   158→  shareId: string,
   159→  keys: Array<{ keyType: 'file' | 'folder'; itemId: string; encryptedKey: string }>
   160→): Promise<void> {
   161→  await sharesControllerAddShareKeys(shareId, {
   162→    keys: keys.map((k) => ({
   163→      keyType: k.keyType,
   164→      itemId: k.itemId,
   165→      encryptedKey: k.encryptedKey,
   166→    })),
   167→  });
   168→}
   169→
   170→/**
   171→ * Get sent shares for a specific item (by IPNS name).
   172→ * Fetches all sent shares and filters by ipnsName.
   173→ * Uses the store cache if available and fresh.
   174→ */
   175→export async function getSentSharesForItem(ipnsName: string): Promise<SentShare[]> {
   176→  const shares = await ensureFreshSentShares();
   177→  return shares.filter((s) => s.ipnsName === ipnsName);
   178→}
   179→
   180→// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   181→// Post-upload / post-create share key propagation
   182→// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   183→
   184→/**
   185→ * Ensure sent shares cache is fresh (fetched within last 30s).
   186→ * Returns the current sent shares array.
   187→ */
   188→async function ensureFreshSentShares(): Promise<SentShare[]> {
   189→  const store = useShareStore.getState();
   190→  if (store.lastFetchedAt && Date.now() - store.lastFetchedAt < 30_000) {
   191→    return store.sentShares;
   192→  }
   193→  const shares = await fetchSentShares();
   194→  useShareStore.getState().setSentShares(shares);
   195→  return shares;
   196→}
   197→
   198→/**
   199→ * Check if a folder (by IPNS name) has any active shares.
   200→ * Used to decide whether post-upload re-wrapping is needed.
   201→ */
   202→export async function hasActiveShares(folderIpnsName: string): Promise<boolean> {
   203→  const shares = await ensureFreshSentShares();
   204→  return shares.some((s) => s.ipnsName === folderIpnsName);
   205→}
   206→
   207→/**
   208→ * Find active shares that cover a given folder, including ancestor shares.
   209→ * A folder is "covered" if it or any of its ancestor folders is shared.
   210→ *
   211→ * Walks the ancestor chain and checks each folder's IPNS name against sent shares.
   212→ *
   213→ * @param folderIpnsName - IPNS name of the current folder
   214→ * @param folders - Current folder tree from the folder store
   215→ * @param currentFolderId - ID of the current folder in the tree
   216→ * @returns Array of sent shares covering this folder (may be from ancestor)
   217→ */
   218→export async function findCoveringShares(
   219→  folderIpnsName: string,
   220→  folders: Record<string, FolderNode>,
   221→  currentFolderId: string | null
   222→): Promise<SentShare[]> {
   223→  const shares = await ensureFreshSentShares();
   224→  if (shares.length === 0) return [];
   225→
   226→  // Collect all IPNS names from this folder up to root
   227→  const ipnsNames = new Set<string>();
   228→  ipnsNames.add(folderIpnsName);
   229→
   230→  let walkId = currentFolderId;
   231→  while (walkId) {
   232→    const node = folders[walkId];
   233→    if (!node) break;
   234→    ipnsNames.add(node.ipnsName);
   235→    walkId = node.parentId;
   236→  }
   237→
   238→  return shares.filter((s) => ipnsNames.has(s.ipnsName));
   239→}
   240→
   241→/**
   242→ * After adding a file or subfolder to a shared folder, re-wrap the new key
   243→ * for all existing share recipients.
   244→ *
   245→ * This is a fire-and-forget operation -- failures are logged but don't block
   246→ * the primary upload/create flow.
   247→ *
   248→ * IMPORTANT: Callers are responsible for zeroing `newItems[*].plaintextKey`
   249→ * after this function completes. This function does NOT zero the keys because
   250→ * some callers (e.g., subfolder creation) keep the key alive in the store.
   251→ * Use a `finally` block to ensure zeroing even on errors.
   252→ *
   253→ * @param params.folderIpnsName - IPNS name of the folder being modified
   254→ * @param params.folders - Current folder tree from the store
   255→ * @param params.currentFolderId - ID of the current folder in the tree
   256→ * @param params.newItems - New items whose keys need re-wrapping
   257→ */
   258→export async function reWrapForRecipients(params: {
   259→  folderIpnsName: string;
   260→  folders: Record<string, FolderNode>;
   261→  currentFolderId: string | null;
   262→  newItems: Array<{
   263→    keyType: 'file' | 'folder';
   264→    itemId: string;
   265→    plaintextKey: Uint8Array;
   266→  }>;
   267→}): Promise<void> {
   268→  const coveringShares = await findCoveringShares(
   269→    params.folderIpnsName,
   270→    params.folders,
   271→    params.currentFolderId
   272→  );
   273→
   274→  if (coveringShares.length === 0) return;
   275→
   276→  // For each share recipient, re-wrap all new item keys
   277→  for (const share of coveringShares) {
   278→    try {
   279→      const recipientPubKey = hexToBytes(
   280→        share.recipientPublicKey.startsWith('0x')
   281→          ? share.recipientPublicKey.slice(2)
   282→          : share.recipientPublicKey
   283→      );
   284→
   285→      const wrappedKeys: Array<{
   286→        keyType: 'file' | 'folder';
   287→        itemId: string;
   288→        encryptedKey: string;
   289→      }> = [];
   290→
   291→      for (const item of params.newItems) {
   292→        const wrapped = await wrapKey(item.plaintextKey, recipientPubKey);
   293→        wrappedKeys.push({
   294→          keyType: item.keyType,
   295→          itemId: item.itemId,
   296→          encryptedKey: bytesToHex(wrapped),
   297→        });
   298→      }
   299→
   300→      // Add the wrapped keys to this share via API
   301→      await addShareKeys(share.shareId, wrappedKeys);
   302→    } catch (err) {
   303→      console.warn(
   304→        `[share] Failed to re-wrap keys for recipient ${share.recipientPublicKey.slice(0, 10)}...:`,
   305→        err
   306→      );
   307→    }
   308→  }
   309→}
   310→
   311→// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   312→// Lazy key rotation after revocation
   313→// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   314→
   315→/** A revoked share pending key rotation. */
   316→export type PendingRotation = {
   317→  shareId: string;
   318→  recipientPublicKey: string;
   319→  itemType: 'folder' | 'file';
   320→  ipnsName: string;
   321→  itemName: string;
   322→  revokedAt: string;
   323→};
   324→
   325→/**
   326→ * Fetch revoked shares that are pending key rotation from the server.
   327→ * These are shares where revokedAt is set but the share has not been hard-deleted.
   328→ */
   329→export async function fetchPendingRotations(): Promise<PendingRotation[]> {
   330→  const response = await sharesControllerGetPendingRotations();
   331→
   332→  return response.map((r) => ({
   333→    shareId: r.shareId,
   334→    recipientPublicKey: r.recipientPublicKey,
   335→    itemType: r.itemType as 'folder' | 'file',
   336→    ipnsName: r.ipnsName,
   337→    itemName: r.itemName,
   338→    revokedAt: String(r.revokedAt),
   339→  }));
   340→}
   341→
   342→/**
   343→ * Check if a folder has pending rotations (revoked shares awaiting key rotation).
   344→ * Called before any folder modification.
   345→ *
   346→ * @param folderIpnsName - IPNS name of the folder being modified
   347→ * @returns true if there are revoked shares for this folder that need rotation
   348→ */
   349→export async function checkPendingRotation(folderIpnsName: string): Promise<boolean> {
   350→  const pendingRotations = await fetchPendingRotations();
   351→  return pendingRotations.some((r) => r.ipnsName === folderIpnsName);
   352→}
   353→
   354→/**
   355→ * Update the encrypted key on a share record after lazy key rotation.
   356→ * Re-wraps the new folder key for a remaining (non-revoked) recipient.
   357→ */
   358→export async function updateShareKey(shareId: string, encryptedKey: string): Promise<void> {
   359→  await sharesControllerUpdateShareEncryptedKey(shareId, { encryptedKey });
   360→}
   361→
   362→/**
   363→ * Hard-delete a revoked share after rotation is complete.
   364→ */
   365→export async function completeShareRotation(shareId: string): Promise<void> {
   366→  await sharesControllerCompleteRotation(shareId);
   367→}
   368→
   369→/**
   370→ * Execute lazy key rotation for a folder.
   371→ * Called when a folder modification is about to happen and pending rotations exist.
   372→ *
   373→ * Protocol:
   374→ * 1. Generate new random folderKey
   375→ * 2. Re-wrap new folderKey for each REMAINING (non-revoked) active recipient
   376→ * 3. Update remaining shares with the new encrypted key
   377→ * 4. Hard-delete revoked share records (rotation complete)
   378→ * 5. Invalidate share cache
   379→ *
   380→ * NOTE: The actual folder metadata re-encryption (decrypt with old key, re-encrypt
   381→ * with new key, re-publish IPNS) is handled by the caller (folder.service.ts)
   382→ * since it has access to the folder's IPNS private key and publishing infrastructure.
   383→ *
   384→ * @returns The new folderKey for the caller to use
   385→ */
   386→export async function executeLazyRotation(params: {
   387→  folderIpnsName: string;
   388→  oldFolderKey: Uint8Array;
   389→  ownerPublicKey: Uint8Array;
   390→}): Promise<{ newFolderKey: Uint8Array }> {
   391→  // 1. Generate new random 32-byte folderKey
   392→  const newFolderKey = generateRandomBytes(32);
   393→
   394→  // 2. Fetch pending rotations and active shares for this folder
   395→  const [pendingRotations, activeSentShares] = await Promise.all([
   396→    fetchPendingRotations(),
   397→    getSentSharesForItem(params.folderIpnsName),
   398→  ]);
   399→
   400→  const revokedForFolder = pendingRotations.filter((r) => r.ipnsName === params.folderIpnsName);
   401→  const revokedShareIds = new Set(revokedForFolder.map((r) => r.shareId));
   402→
   403→  // Active shares that are NOT revoked -- these recipients keep access
   404→  const remainingShares = activeSentShares.filter((s) => !revokedShareIds.has(s.shareId));
   405→
   406→  // 3. Re-wrap new folderKey for each remaining recipient
   407→  const reWrapFailures: string[] = [];
   408→  for (const share of remainingShares) {
   409→    try {
   410→      const recipientPubKey = hexToBytes(
   411→        share.recipientPublicKey.startsWith('0x')
   412→          ? share.recipientPublicKey.slice(2)
   413→          : share.recipientPublicKey
   414→      );
   415→      const wrapped = await wrapKey(newFolderKey, recipientPubKey);
   416→      await updateShareKey(share.shareId, bytesToHex(wrapped));
   417→    } catch (err) {
   418→      console.warn(
   419→        `[share] Failed to update share key for remaining recipient ${share.recipientPublicKey.slice(0, 10)}...:`,
   420→        err
   421→      );
   422→      reWrapFailures.push(share.shareId);
   423→    }
   424→  }
   425→
   426→  if (reWrapFailures.length > 0) {
   427→    throw new Error(
   428→      `Key rotation failed: could not re-wrap key for ${reWrapFailures.length} recipient(s). ` +
   429→        'Aborting to prevent inconsistent state.'
   430→    );
   431→  }
   432→
   433→  // 4. Hard-delete all revoked shares for this folder
   434→  for (const revoked of revokedForFolder) {
   435→    try {
   436→      await completeShareRotation(revoked.shareId);
   437→    } catch (err) {
   438→      console.warn(`[share] Failed to complete rotation for share ${revoked.shareId}:`, err);
   439→    }
   440→  }
   441→
   442→  // 5. Invalidate the sent shares cache so next check fetches fresh state
   443→  useShareStore.getState().setSentShares([]);
   444→
   445→  return { newFolderKey };
   446→}
   447→

   980→
   981→  const auth = useAuthStore.getState();
   982→  if (!auth.vaultKeypair) {
   983→    // Cannot rotate without owner's keypair -- skip rotation, use existing key
   984→    console.warn('[share] Cannot perform lazy rotation: no vault keypair available');
   985→    return { folderKey: folderNode.folderKey, rotated: false };
   986→  }
   987→
   988→  // 2. Execute lazy rotation: generates new key, re-wraps for remaining recipients,
   989→  //    hard-deletes revoked share records
   990→  const { newFolderKey } = await executeLazyRotation({
   991→    folderIpnsName: folderNode.ipnsName,
   992→    oldFolderKey: folderNode.folderKey,
   993→    ownerPublicKey: auth.vaultKeypair.publicKey,
   994→  });
   995→
   996→  // 3. Re-encrypt folder metadata with the new key
   997→  //    Resolve current IPNS -> fetch encrypted metadata -> decrypt with old key -> re-encrypt with new key
   998→  const resolved = await resolveIpnsRecord(folderNode.ipnsName);
   999→  if (!resolved) {
  1000→    // IPNS resolution failed after rotation was committed (share keys already re-wrapped,
  1001→    // revoked shares hard-deleted). Metadata is still encrypted with the old key.
  1002→    // Throw so the caller doesn't proceed with the new key in an inconsistent state.
  1003→    console.error(
  1004→      `[share] IPNS resolution failed after lazy rotation for ${folderNode.ipnsName}. ` +
  1005→        'Share keys were updated but metadata was not re-encrypted.'
  1006→    );
  1007→    throw new Error(
  1008→      'Key rotation failed: could not resolve folder IPNS for metadata re-encryption'
  1009→    );
  1010→  }
  1011→
  1012→  const currentMetadata = await fetchAndDecryptMetadata(resolved.cid, folderNode.folderKey);
  1013→
  1014→  // Re-encrypt and publish with new key
  1015→  await updateFolderMetadata({
  1016→    folderId: folderNode.id,
  1017→    children: currentMetadata.children ?? [],
  1018→    folderKey: newFolderKey,
  1019→    ipnsPrivateKey: folderNode.ipnsPrivateKey,
  1020→    ipnsName: folderNode.ipnsName,
  1021→    sequenceNumber: folderNode.sequenceNumber,
  1022→  });
  1023→
  1024→  // 4. Re-wrap new folderKey in parent metadata
  1025→  //    The parent needs its child's folderKeyEncrypted updated.
  1026→  //    This is done by the caller since they have access to the parent folder.
  1027→  //    We just return the new key and the rotated flag.
  1028→
  1029→  return { folderKey: newFolderKey, rotated: true };
  1030→}
  1031→

  "old_string": "async function reWrapEncryptedKey(\n  encryptedKeyHex: string,\n  ownerPrivateKey: Uint8Array,\n  recipientPubKey: Uint8Array\n): Promise<string> {\n  const plainKey = await unwrapKey(hexToBytes(encryptedKeyHex), ownerPrivateKey);\n  const rewrapped = await wrapKey(plainKey, recipientPubKey);\n  plainKey.fill(0);\n  return bytesToHex(rewrapped);\n}",
  "new_string": "async function reWrapEncryptedKey(\n  encryptedKeyHex: string,\n  ownerPrivateKey: Uint8Array,\n  recipientPubKey: Uint8Array\n): Promise<string> {\n  const plainKey = await unwrapKey(hexToBytes(encryptedKeyHex), ownerPrivateKey);\n  try {\n    const rewrapped = await wrapKey(plainKey, recipientPubKey);\n    return bytesToHex(rewrapped);\n  } finally {\n    plainKey.fill(0);\n  }\n}"
}

     1→import { test, expect, Browser } from '@playwright/test';
     2→import {
     3→  createTestAccount,
     4→  closeTestAccounts,
     5→  navigateToShared,
     6→  navigateToFiles,
     7→  type TestAccount,
     8→} from '../utils/multi-account';
     9→import { createTestTextFile, cleanupTestFiles } from '../utils/test-files';
    10→import { FileListPage } from '../page-objects/file-browser/file-list.page';
    11→import { UploadZonePage } from '../page-objects/file-browser/upload-zone.page';
    12→import { ContextMenuPage } from '../page-objects/file-browser/context-menu.page';
    13→import { CreateFolderDialogPage } from '../page-objects/dialogs/create-folder-dialog.page';
    14→import { ShareDialogPage } from '../page-objects/dialogs/share-dialog.page';
    15→import { SharedFileBrowserPage } from '../page-objects/file-browser/shared-file-browser.page';
    16→
    17→/**
    18→ * Sharing Workflow E2E Test Suite
    19→ *
    20→ * Tests user-to-user sharing with multiple authenticated accounts.
    21→ * Each test account gets its own browser context (isolated cookies/storage).
    22→ *
    23→ * Accounts:
    24→ * - Alice: primary sharer (creates content and shares it)
    25→ * - Bob: primary recipient (receives and browses shared content)
    26→ * - Charlie: secondary recipient (for multi-recipient tests)
    27→ *
    28→ * Test flow:
    29→ * 1. Setup: Create 3 accounts, Alice creates test content
    30→ * 2. Basic sharing: Alice shares file with Bob, Bob receives it
    31→ * 3. Folder sharing: Alice shares folder tree, Bob navigates it
    32→ * 4. Multi-recipient: Alice adds Charlie to existing share
    33→ * 5. Revocation: Alice revokes Bob's access
    34→ * 6. Post-share mutations: Alice adds files to shared folder
    35→ * 7. Error cases: Invalid key, self-share, user not found
    36→ * 8. Hide share: Bob hides a shared item
    37→ */
    38→test.describe.serial('Sharing Workflow', () => {
    39→  let browser: Browser;
    40→  let alice: TestAccount;
    41→  let bob: TestAccount;
    42→  let charlie: TestAccount;
    43→
    44→  // Page objects per account
    45→  let aliceFileList: FileListPage;
    46→  let aliceUploadZone: UploadZonePage;
    47→  let aliceContextMenu: ContextMenuPage;
    48→  let aliceCreateFolderDialog: CreateFolderDialogPage;
    49→  let aliceShareDialog: ShareDialogPage;
    50→
    51→  let bobSharedBrowser: SharedFileBrowserPage;
    52→  let bobContextMenu: ContextMenuPage;
    53→
    54→  let charlieSharedBrowser: SharedFileBrowserPage;
    55→
    56→  // Test data - unique per run
    57→  const runId = Date.now().toString();
    58→
    59→  // Shared file
    60→  const sharedFileName = `shared-file-${runId}.txt`;
    61→  const sharedFileContent = 'This file is shared with Bob';
    62→
    63→  // Shared folder structure
    64→  const sharedFolderName = `shared-folder-${runId}`;
    65→  const subFolderName = `subfolder-${runId}`;
    66→  const folderFile1Name = `folder-file1-${runId}.txt`;
    67→  const folderFile2Name = `subfolder-file-${runId}.txt`;
    68→
    69→  // Post-share file (added after share is created)
    70→  const postShareFileName = `added-after-share-${runId}.txt`;
    71→  const postShareFileContent = 'This file was added after the share was created';
    72→
    73→  // Helper to truncate a public key for matching in the UI
    74→  function truncateKey(key: string): string {
    75→    const hex = key.startsWith('0x') ? key.slice(2) : key;
    76→    return `0x${hex.slice(0, 4)}...${hex.slice(-4)}`;
    77→  }
    78→
    79→  // ============================================
    80→  // Alice helpers
    81→  // ============================================
    82→
    83→  async function aliceCreateFolder(name: string): Promise<void> {
    84→    const newFolderButton = alice.page.locator('.file-browser-new-folder-button');
    85→    await newFolderButton.click();
    86→    await aliceCreateFolderDialog.waitForOpen();
    87→    await aliceCreateFolderDialog.createFolder(name);
    88→    await aliceFileList.waitForItemToAppear(name, { timeout: 30000 });
    89→  }
    90→
    91→  async function aliceUploadFile(fileName: string, content: string): Promise<void> {
    92→    const testFile = createTestTextFile(fileName, content);
    93→    await aliceUploadZone.uploadFile(testFile.path);
    94→
    95→    // Wait for upload: check for either the file appearing or an error alert
    96→    await Promise.race([
    97→      aliceFileList.waitForItemToAppear(fileName, { timeout: 30000 }),
    98→      alice.page
    99→        .locator('[role="alert"]')
   100→        .waitFor({ state: 'visible', timeout: 30000 })
   101→        .then(async () => {
   102→          const alertText = await alice.page.locator('[role="alert"]').textContent();
   103→          throw new Error(`Upload failed with alert: ${alertText}`);
   104→        }),
   105→    ]);
   106→  }
   107→
   108→  async function aliceNavigateIntoFolder(name: string): Promise<void> {
   109→    await aliceFileList.doubleClickFolder(name);
   110→    // Wait for breadcrumbs to show the folder name
   111→    await alice.page.locator('.breadcrumb-item', { hasText: name.toLowerCase() }).waitFor({
   112→      state: 'visible',
   113→      timeout: 30000,
   114→    });
   115→  }
   116→
   117→  async function aliceNavigateBack(): Promise<void> {
   118→    await alice.page.locator('[data-testid="parent-dir-row"]').dblclick();
   119→    await alice.page.waitForTimeout(500);
   120→  }
   121→
   122→  async function aliceOpenShareDialog(itemName: string): Promise<void> {
   123→    await aliceFileList.rightClickItem(itemName);
   124→    await aliceContextMenu.waitForOpen();
   125→    await aliceContextMenu.clickShare();
   126→    await aliceShareDialog.waitForOpen();
   127→    await aliceShareDialog.waitForRecipientsLoaded();
   128→  }
   129→
   130→  // ============================================
   131→  // Setup and Teardown
   132→  // ============================================
   133→
   134→  test.beforeAll(async ({ browser: testBrowser }) => {
   135→    browser = testBrowser;
   136→  });
   137→
   138→  test.afterAll(async () => {

   140→    if (alice || bob || charlie) {

   144→
   145→  // ============================================
   146→  // Phase 1: Account Setup
   147→  // ============================================
   148→
   149→  test('1.1 Create test accounts (Alice, Bob, Charlie)', async () => {
   150→    // Create accounts sequentially (each needs its own context + vault init)
   151→    alice = await createTestAccount(browser, 'alice', runId);
   152→    bob = await createTestAccount(browser, 'bob', runId);
   153→    charlie = await createTestAccount(browser, 'charlie', runId);
   154→
   155→    // Initialize page objects for Alice

   158→    aliceContextMenu = new ContextMenuPage(alice.page);

   162→    // Initialize page objects for Bob

   165→

     1→import { Browser, BrowserContext, Page } from '@playwright/test';

     4→/**
     5→ * A fully authenticated test account with its own browser context.
     6→ * Each account has an isolated session (cookies, localStorage, auth state).
     7→ */
     8→export interface TestAccount {
     9→  name: string;
    10→  email: string;
    11→  context: BrowserContext;
    12→  page: Page;

    18→/**
    19→ * Create a test account with a unique email and authenticated browser context.
    20→ *
    21→ * Uses the test-login endpoint to bypass Web3Auth. Each account gets:
    22→ * - A unique timestamped email for test isolation
    23→ * - Its own BrowserContext (isolated cookies/storage)
    24→ * - A deterministic secp256k1 keypair (derived from email)
    25→ * - A fully initialized vault
    26→ *
    27→ * @param browser - Playwright browser instance
    28→ * @param name - Human-readable name for this account (e.g., "alice")
    29→ * @param runId - Unique identifier for this test run (timestamp)
    30→ */
    31→export async function createTestAccount(
    32→  browser: Browser,
    33→  name: string,
    34→  runId: string
    35→): Promise<TestAccount> {
    36→  const email = `${name}-e2e-${runId}@test.local`;
    37→  const context = await browser.newContext();
    38→  const page = await context.newPage();
    39→
    40→  const authState = await loginViaTestEndpoint(page, email);
    41→  const publicKey = getPublicKeyHex(authState);
    42→
    43→  return { name, email, context, page, authState, publicKey };
    44→}
    45→
    46→/**
    47→ * Create multiple test accounts in parallel.
    48→ * All accounts share the same runId for test isolation.
    49→ */
    50→export async function createTestAccounts(
    51→  browser: Browser,
    52→  names: string[],
    53→  runId?: string
    54→): Promise<TestAccount[]> {
    55→  const id = runId ?? Date.now().toString();
    56→  // Create accounts sequentially to avoid overwhelming the API
    57→  const accounts: TestAccount[] = [];
    58→  for (const name of names) {
    59→    accounts.push(await createTestAccount(browser, name, id));
    60→  }
    61→  return accounts;
    62→}
    63→

    68→export async function closeTestAccounts(accounts: TestAccount[]): Promise<void> {
    69→  for (const account of accounts) {
    70→    await account.context.close();

Now I have the complete picture. Here is my full analysis.

---

## Investigation Report: Sharing Workflow E2E Test Failures

### 1. CRITICAL ISSUE: Public Key Format Mismatch (`0x` prefix missing)

**Location:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/auth/auth.service.ts`, line 584

The `generateDeterministicKeypair` method in the auth service produces a **bare hex** public key without `0x` prefix:

```typescript
const publicKeyHex = ecdh.getPublicKey().toString('hex'); // 65 bytes uncompressed
// Returns: "04ab12cd..." (no 0x prefix)
```

This bare hex value is:
- Stored in the database as `user.publicKey`
- Returned from the `/auth/test-login` endpoint as `publicKeyHex`
- Used as `authState.publicKeyHex` in the E2E test helpers

**But** the shares lookup endpoint at `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/shares/shares.controller.ts`, line 155, validates with a regex requiring the `0x04` prefix:

```typescript
if (!publicKey || !/^0x04[0-9a-fA-F]{128}$/.test(publicKey)) {
  throw new BadRequestException(
    'Invalid public key format. Expected uncompressed secp256k1 key: 0x04 + 128 hex chars'
  );
}
```

And `lookupUserByPublicKey` (line 247 of shares.service.ts) does an exact match against `user.publicKey` in the database:

```typescript
const user = await this.userRepo.findOne({
  where: { publicKey },  // stored as "04ab..." but queried as "0x04ab..."
});
```

**The chain of failures:**

1. `testLogin` stores `publicKey = "04abcd..."` (no `0x` prefix) in the `users` table.
2. The test's `TestAccount.publicKey` gets the value `"04abcd..."` from `getPublicKeyHex(authState)` which just returns `authState.publicKeyHex`.
3. When Alice tries to share with Bob using `bob.publicKey` (`"04abcd..."`), the ShareDialog UI's `isValidPublicKey()` check requires `0x04` prefix and rejects it as "invalid key format".
4. Even if the test manually prepends `0x`, the lookup endpoint would search for `publicKey = "0x04abcd..."` but the DB has `"04abcd..."` -- so it would return "user not found".

**This is the root cause.** There's a two-way mismatch:
- The key stored in DB lacks `0x`, but the lookup regex requires `0x`
- The key returned to E2E tests lacks `0x`, but the ShareDialog UI requires `0x04`

**Fix needed:** Either:
- (a) `generateDeterministicKeypair` should return `publicKeyHex` with `0x` prefix: `'0x' + ecdh.getPublicKey().toString('hex')`, OR
- (b) The `getPublicKeyHex` helper in `web3auth-helpers.ts` should prepend `0x` if missing, AND the lookup endpoint should strip `0x` before querying the DB.

Option (a) is cleaner, but requires migrating existing test users in the DB. Option (b) is safer as a targeted fix.

### 2. `truncatePubkey` vs `truncateKey` -- Subtle Display Mismatch

**Test code** (`sharing-workflow.spec.ts`, line 74):
```typescript
function truncateKey(key: string): string {
  const hex = key.startsWith('0x') ? key.slice(2) : key;
  return `0x${hex.slice(0, 4)}...${hex.slice(-4)}`;
}
// "04abcd...ef12" -> "0x04ab...ef12"
```

**SharedFileBrowser UI** (`SharedFileBrowser.tsx`, line 84):
```typescript
function truncatePubkey(pubkey: string): string {
  if (pubkey.length <= 12) return pubkey;
  const prefix = pubkey.startsWith('0x') ? pubkey.slice(0, 6) : pubkey.slice(0, 4);
  const suffix = pubkey.slice(-4);
  return `${prefix}...${suffix}`;
}
```

For a key **without** `0x` prefix (like `"04abcd..."` from the DB):
- Test's `truncateKey`: `"0x04ab...ef12"`
- UI's `truncatePubkey`: `"04ab...ef12"` (no `0x` prefix)

For a key **with** `0x` prefix (like `"0x04abcd..."`):
- Test's `truncateKey`: `"0x04ab...ef12"`  
- UI's `truncatePubkey`: `"0x04ab...ef12"` (matches -- both take first 6 chars after detecting `0x`)

This means even if issue #1 is fixed, the test assertion at line 244 (`expect(sharedBy).toContain(truncateKey(alice.publicKey))`) would fail **unless** the public key consistently uses the `0x` prefix throughout. So the fix for issue #1 also resolves this.

### 3. Test-Login Endpoint: Exists and Is Functional

**Status:** The `/auth/test-login` endpoint exists at `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/auth/auth.controller.ts`, line 222. It is properly guarded by `TEST_LOGIN_SECRET` environment variable, rate-limited, and blocked in production mode. The `TEST_LOGIN_SECRET` is configured in the E2E `.env` file.

### 4. ShareDialog Page Object vs UI Component: Selectors Match

All CSS class selectors in `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/e2e/page-objects/dialogs/share-dialog.page.ts` correctly match the ShareDialog component at `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/web/src/components/file-browser/ShareDialog.tsx`:

### 5. SharedFileBrowser Page Object vs UI Component: Selectors Match

All selectors in `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/e2e/page-objects/file-browser/shared-file-browser.page.ts` match the SharedFileBrowser component:

All selectors are correct.

### 6. Context Menu Page Object: Selectors Match

The ContextMenu page object at `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/e2e/page-objects/file-browser/context-menu.page.ts` uses `role="menu"` and `role="menuitem"` selectors, which match the component at `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/web/src/components/file-browser/ContextMenu.tsx`. The `Share` and `Hide` menu items are both present in the component and the page object.

One note: the `Hide` option is only rendered when `readOnly && onHide` is truthy (line 373 in ContextMenu.tsx). In the SharedFileBrowser's top-level list view, `onHide` is passed when `contextShareId` is set (line 321), but in the folder view (line 497), `onHide` is **not** passed. This means the "Hide" context menu option only works on the top-level shared items list -- which is the correct behavior for test 9.1.

### 7. SharedFileBrowser: `navigateIntoFolder` Uses Single Click

The page object's `navigateIntoFolder` method (line 178) calls `openSharedItem(name)` which does a **single click** (line 142). In the UI, the `SharedListRow` triggers `onOpen` (which calls `navigateToShare(shareId)`) on both `onClick` and `onDoubleClick` (lines 583-584). So a single click works. This is correct.

However, inside a folder, `doubleClickFolderItem` calls `dblclick()` on `getFolderItem(name)`. The `SharedFolderRow` handles `onDoubleClick` (line 638) separately from `onClick` (line 637). The `onClick` triggers `onOpen` which only navigates for folders. The `onDoubleClick` navigates for folders and downloads for files. Both should work.

### Summary of Issues Found

| # | Severity | Issue | Location |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **CRITICAL** | Public key from `generateDeterministicKeypair` lacks `0x` prefix, but the shares lookup endpoint requires `0x04` prefix and the ShareDialog UI requires `0x04` prefix. Additionally, DB stores bare hex but lookup queries with `0x` prefix. | `auth.service.ts:584`, `shares.controller.ts:155`, `ShareDialog.tsx:49` |
| 2 | **MEDIUM** | `truncatePubkey` in UI produces different output than `truncateKey` in tests when key lacks `0x` prefix. Resolves itself when issue #1 is fixed. | `SharedFileBrowser.tsx:84` vs `sharing-workflow.spec.ts:74` |

Issues 1 and 2 are related -- fixing the key format consistency (adding `0x` prefix to `generateDeterministicKeypair` output) would resolve both. The recommended fix is to change line 584 of `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/api/src/auth/auth.service.ts` from:

```typescript
const publicKeyHex = ecdh.getPublicKey().toString('hex');
```

to:

```typescript
const publicKeyHex = '0x' + ecdh.getPublicKey().toString('hex');
```

This would make the test-login endpoint produce keys in the `0x04...` format that the rest of the system expects. Existing test users in the DB would need to be re-created (or a migration run to add the `0x` prefix to their stored public keys).

2. The E2E `getPublicKeyHex` should prepend `0x` to match UI expectations

     1→import { Page } from '@playwright/test';
     2→import {
     3→  initializeVault,

    10→import * as ed from '@noble/ed25519';
    11→
    12→/**
    13→ * Test credentials from environment variables.
    14→ * These are loaded from .env locally or GitHub Secrets in CI.
    15→ *
    16→ * With Core Kit, the E2E flow uses CipherBox's own login UI:
    17→ * 1. Enter email in CipherBox email input
    18→ * 2. Backend sends OTP (static OTP in test mode)
    19→ * 3. Enter OTP code
    20→ * 4. CipherBox JWT -> Core Kit loginWithJWT -> backend auth -> vault init
    21→ */
    22→export const TEST_CREDENTIALS = {
    23→  email: process.env.WEB3AUTH_TEST_EMAIL || '',
    24→  otp: process.env.WEB3AUTH_TEST_OTP || '',
    25→};
    26→
    27→/**
    28→ * Serializable auth state for a test account.
    29→ * Arrays are used because Uint8Array can't cross the page.evaluate boundary.
    30→ */
    31→export interface TestAuthState {
    32→  accessToken: string;
    33→  email: string;
    34→  publicKeyHex: string;
    35→  publicKeyArr: number[];
    36→  privateKeyArr: number[];
    37→  rootFolderKeyArr: number[];
    38→  rootIpnsPublicKeyArr: number[];
    39→  rootIpnsPrivateKeyArr: number[];
    40→  rootIpnsName: string;
    41→  vaultId: string;
    42→}
    43→
    44→/**
    45→ * Cached auth state from test-login endpoint (singleton for backward compat).
    46→ * Used to re-inject state after page reload (since test-login bypasses
    47→ * Core Kit, there's no persistent session to restore from).
    48→ */
    49→let cachedTestAuthState: TestAuthState | null = null;
    50→
    51→/**
    52→ * Performs email login through CipherBox's custom login UI.
    53→ *
    54→ * When TEST_LOGIN_SECRET is set, uses the /auth/test-login endpoint to bypass
    55→ * Core Kit entirely. This decouples E2E tests from Web3Auth infrastructure.
    56→ *
    57→ * Otherwise falls back to the full Core Kit flow through CipherBox's login UI.
    58→ *
    59→ * @param page - Playwright page instance
    60→ * @param email - Email address for login
    61→ * @param otp - Optional OTP code (defaults to TEST_CREDENTIALS.otp)
    62→ */
    63→export async function loginViaEmail(page: Page, email: string, otp?: string): Promise<void> {
    64→  // Use test-login endpoint when available (bypasses Core Kit completely)
    65→  if (process.env.TEST_LOGIN_SECRET) {
    66→    const state = await loginViaTestEndpoint(page, email);
    67→    cachedTestAuthState = state;
    68→    return;
    69→  }
    70→
    71→  const otpCode = otp || TEST_CREDENTIALS.otp;
    72→
    73→  // Navigate to login page
    74→  await page.goto('/');
    75→
    76→  // Step 1: Enter email in CipherBox's own email input
    77→  const emailInput = page.locator('[data-testid="email-input"]');
    78→  await emailInput.waitFor({ state: 'visible', timeout: 15000 });
    79→  await emailInput.fill(email);
    80→
    81→  // Step 2: Click [SEND OTP] button
    82→  const sendOtpButton = page.locator('[data-testid="send-otp-button"]');
    83→  await sendOtpButton.click();
    84→
    85→  // Step 3: Wait for OTP input to appear (backend sends OTP, form transitions)
    86→  const otpInput = page.locator('[data-testid="otp-input"]');
    87→  await otpInput.waitFor({ state: 'visible', timeout: 15000 });
    88→
    89→  // Step 4: Enter the test OTP code
    90→  await otpInput.fill(otpCode);
    91→
    92→  // Step 5: Click [VERIFY] button
    93→  const verifyButton = page.locator('[data-testid="verify-button"]');
    94→  await verifyButton.click();
    95→
    96→  // Step 6: Wait for login to complete and redirect to files page
    97→  // Core Kit loginWithJWT + backend auth + vault init can take time
    98→  await page.waitForURL('**/files', { timeout: 60000 });
    99→}
   100→
   101→/**
   102→ * Test-login endpoint flow that bypasses Core Kit entirely.
   103→ *
   104→ * 1. Calls POST /auth/test-login -> tokens + deterministic secp256k1 keypair
   105→ * 2. Handles vault init/load using @cipherbox/crypto in Node.js
   106→ * 3. Injects all state into Zustand stores via page.evaluate
   107→ * 4. Navigates to /files
   108→ *
   109→ * Returns the auth state so callers can manage per-account state
   110→ * (e.g., for multi-account sharing tests).
   111→ */
   112→export async function loginViaTestEndpoint(page: Page, email: string): Promise<TestAuthState> {
   113→  const apiBase = process.env.API_BASE_URL || 'http://localhost:3000';
   114→  const secret = process.env.TEST_LOGIN_SECRET!;
   115→
   116→  // 1. Authenticate via test-login endpoint
   117→  const loginResponse = await page.request.post(`${apiBase}/auth/test-login`, {
   118→    data: { email, secret },
   119→  });
   120→
   121→  if (!loginResponse.ok()) {
   122→    const body = await loginResponse.text();
   123→    throw new Error(`Test login failed (${loginResponse.status()}): ${body}`);
   124→  }
   125→
   126→  const { accessToken, refreshToken, publicKeyHex, privateKeyHex } = await loginResponse.json();
   127→  const publicKey = hexToBytes(publicKeyHex);
   128→  const privateKey = hexToBytes(privateKeyHex);
   129→
   130→  // 2. Handle vault: load existing or initialize new
   131→  const vaultResponse = await page.request.get(`${apiBase}/vault`, {
   132→    headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${accessToken}` },
   133→  });
   134→
   135→  let vaultId: string;
   136→  let rootFolderKey: Uint8Array;
   137→  let rootIpnsPrivateKey: Uint8Array;
   138→  let rootIpnsName: string;
   139→
   140→  if (vaultResponse.ok()) {
   141→    // Existing vault — decrypt keys
   142→    const vault = await vaultResponse.json();
   143→    vaultId = vault.id;
   144→    rootIpnsName = vault.rootIpnsName;
   145→
   146→    const decrypted = await decryptVaultKeys(
   147→      {
   148→        encryptedRootFolderKey: hexToBytes(vault.encryptedRootFolderKey),
   149→        encryptedIpnsPrivateKey: hexToBytes(vault.encryptedRootIpnsPrivateKey),
   150→      },
   151→      privateKey
   152→    );
   153→
   154→    rootFolderKey = decrypted.rootFolderKey;
   155→    rootIpnsPrivateKey = decrypted.rootIpnsKeypair.privateKey;
   156→  } else {
   157→    // New user — initialize vault
   158→    const newVault = await initializeVault(privateKey);
   159→    const encrypted = await encryptVaultKeys(newVault, publicKey);
   160→    rootIpnsName = await deriveIpnsName(newVault.rootIpnsKeypair.publicKey);
   161→
   162→    const initResponse = await page.request.post(`${apiBase}/vault/init`, {
   163→      headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${accessToken}` },
   164→      data: {
   165→        ownerPublicKey: publicKeyHex,
   166→        encryptedRootFolderKey: bytesToHex(encrypted.encryptedRootFolderKey),
   167→        encryptedRootIpnsPrivateKey: bytesToHex(encrypted.encryptedIpnsPrivateKey),
   168→        rootIpnsName,
   169→      },
   170→    });
   171→
   172→    if (!initResponse.ok()) {
   173→      const body = await initResponse.text();
   174→      throw new Error(`Vault init failed (${initResponse.status()}): ${body}`);
   175→    }
   176→
   177→    const initResult = await initResponse.json();
   178→    vaultId = initResult.id;
   179→    rootFolderKey = newVault.rootFolderKey;
   180→    rootIpnsPrivateKey = newVault.rootIpnsKeypair.privateKey;
   181→  }
   182→
   183→  // Derive IPNS public key from private key (deterministic Ed25519 derivation)
   184→  const rootIpnsPublicKey = await ed.getPublicKeyAsync(rootIpnsPrivateKey);
   185→
   186→  const authState: TestAuthState = {
   187→    accessToken,
   188→    email,
   189→    publicKeyHex,
   190→    publicKeyArr: Array.from(publicKey),
   191→    privateKeyArr: Array.from(privateKey),
   192→    rootFolderKeyArr: Array.from(rootFolderKey),
   193→    rootIpnsPublicKeyArr: Array.from(rootIpnsPublicKey),
   194→    rootIpnsPrivateKeyArr: Array.from(rootIpnsPrivateKey),
   195→    rootIpnsName,
   196→    vaultId,
   197→  };
   198→
   199→  // 2.5. Set refresh token cookie in the browser context for session persistence
   200→  const url = new URL(apiBase);
   201→  await page.context().addCookies([
   202→    {
   203→      name: 'refresh_token',
   204→      value: refreshToken,
   205→      domain: url.hostname,
   206→      path: '/auth',
   207→      httpOnly: true,
   208→      secure: false, // dev mode
   209→      sameSite: 'Lax',
   210→    },
   211→  ]);
   212→
   213→  // 3. Navigate to app so we can inject state into Zustand stores
   214→  await page.goto('/');
   215→
   216→  await injectAuthState(page, authState);
   217→
   218→  // 5. Navigate to files page via hash router (no full reload — preserves Zustand state)
   219→  await page.evaluate(() => {
   220→    window.location.hash = '#/files';
   221→  });
   222→  await page.waitForURL('**/files', { timeout: 30000 });
   223→
   224→  return authState;
   225→}
   226→
   227→/**
   228→ * Inject auth state into Zustand stores on a page.
   229→ * Works with both the singleton cache (backward compat) and explicit state (multi-account).
   230→ */
   231→export async function injectAuthState(page: Page, state: TestAuthState): Promise<void> {
   232→  // Wait for the app to load and expose stores (dev mode only)
   233→  await page.waitForFunction(
   234→    () => !!(window as unknown as Record<string, unknown>).__ZUSTAND_STORES,
   235→    {
   236→      timeout: 15000,
   237→    }
   238→  );
   239→
   240→  // Inject auth + vault state into Zustand stores
   241→  // Arrays are used because Uint8Array can't be serialized across page.evaluate boundary
   242→  await page.evaluate((data) => {
   243→    const stores = (window as unknown as Record<string, unknown>).__ZUSTAND_STORES as {
   244→      auth: { setState: (s: Record<string, unknown>) => void };
   245→      vault: { getState: () => { setVaultKeys: (k: Record<string, unknown>) => void } };
   246→    };
   247→
   248→    stores.auth.setState({
   249→      accessToken: data.accessToken,
   250→      isAuthenticated: true,
   251→      lastAuthMethod: 'email_passwordless',
   252→      userEmail: data.email,
   253→      vaultKeypair: {
   254→        publicKey: new Uint8Array(data.publicKeyArr),
   255→        privateKey: new Uint8Array(data.privateKeyArr),
   256→      },
   257→    });
   258→
   259→    stores.vault.getState().setVaultKeys({
   260→      rootFolderKey: new Uint8Array(data.rootFolderKeyArr),
   261→      rootIpnsKeypair: {
   262→        publicKey: new Uint8Array(data.rootIpnsPublicKeyArr),
   263→        privateKey: new Uint8Array(data.rootIpnsPrivateKeyArr),
   264→      },
   265→      rootIpnsName: data.rootIpnsName,
   266→      vaultId: data.vaultId,
   267→    });
   268→  }, state);
   269→}
   270→
   271→/**
   272→ * Re-inject test auth state after a page reload.
   273→ * Since test-login bypasses Core Kit, there's no persistent session —
   274→ * this function restores Zustand stores from the cached first login,
   275→ * refreshes the access token via the HTTP-only cookie, then navigates
   276→ * to /files.
   277→ *
   278→ * Call this after page.reload() in tests that use the test-login flow.
   279→ * For tests using the real Core Kit flow, this is a no-op (Core Kit
   280→ * auto-restores its session).
   281→ *
   282→ * Accepts explicit state for multi-account tests. Falls back to
   283→ * singleton cache for backward compat with existing tests.
   284→ */
   285→export async function reinjectTestAuthAfterReload(
   286→  page: Page,
   287→  state?: TestAuthState
   288→): Promise<void> {
   289→  const authState = state ?? cachedTestAuthState;
   290→
   291→  // If not using test-login, Core Kit handles session restore
   292→  if (!process.env.TEST_LOGIN_SECRET || !authState) return;
   293→
   294→  // Try to refresh the access token via the cookie
   295→  const apiBase = process.env.API_BASE_URL || 'http://localhost:3000';
   296→  const refreshResponse = await page.request.post(`${apiBase}/auth/refresh`);
   297→
   298→  if (refreshResponse.ok()) {
   299→    const { accessToken, email } = await refreshResponse.json();
   300→    authState.accessToken = accessToken;
   301→    if (email) authState.email = email;
   302→  }
   303→
   304→  await injectAuthState(page, authState);
   305→
   306→  // Navigate to files via hash router
   307→  await page.evaluate(() => {
   308→    window.location.hash = '#/files';
   309→  });
   310→  await page.waitForURL('**/files', { timeout: 30000 });
   311→}
   312→
   313→/**
   314→ * Gets the current storage state (cookies and localStorage).
   315→ * Useful for saving authenticated session state.
   316→ */
   317→export async function getStorageState(page: Page): Promise<object> {
   318→  return await page.context().storageState();
   319→}
   320→
   321→/**
   322→ * Waits for the user to be authenticated by checking for auth indicators.
   323→ * This is useful after manual login or when loading storage state.
   324→ */
   325→export async function waitForAuthentication(page: Page): Promise<void> {
   326→  // Wait for user menu to appear (indicator of authenticated state)
   327→  // Phase 6.3: Logout moved to UserMenu dropdown
   328→  await page.waitForSelector('[data-testid="user-menu"]', { timeout: 15000 });
   329→}
   330→
   331→/**
   332→ * Extract the 0x04-prefixed public key hex from a TestAuthState.
   333→ * This is the format needed for the ShareDialog recipient input.
   334→ */
   335→export function getPublicKeyHex(state: TestAuthState): string {
   336→  return state.publicKeyHex;
   337→}
   338→
```

> AGENT

The 1Password SSH agent is consistently failing. Per the memory notes, unsigned commits from GSD agents are acceptable since PRs are squash-merged by GitHub. However, the instructions say not to intentionally bypass signing. You may need to unlock 1Password or retry the commit yourself. The changes are staged and ready — just run:

```
git commit -m "fix(14): normalize public key format for DB queries and E2E tests"
```

Here's a summary of everything done:

## Completed

### CodeRabbit Thread Resolution
- **6 test coverage threads** — replied and resolved (tests existed in commit `68ba6a5`)
- **11 new issue threads** — fixed in code, replied, and resolved
- **All 26 threads** on PR #183 are now resolved

### Code Fixes (committed in `37280ae`)
1. **Removed `recipientId`** from `CreateShareResponseDto` — no longer exposes internal UUIDs
2. **Added `publicKey` format validation** — regex check on lookup endpoint
3. **Added `@ApiResponse(403/409)`** on `completeRotation`
4. **Added `revokedAt` guard** — `completeRotation` now rejects non-revoked shares
5. **Key zeroing: `reWrapEncryptedKey`** — try/finally
6. **Key zeroing: `itemFolderKey`** — try/finally in ShareDialog
7. **Key zeroing: unmount cleanup** — zeroes keys on `useSharedNavigation` unmount
8. **Key zeroing: `itemKey`** — zeroed for file-type shares
9. **Key zeroing: `parentFolderKey`** — try/finally in `downloadSharedFileFromShare`
10. **Key zeroing: `newFolderKey`** — zeroed before throwing on rotation failure
11. **IPNS sequence number** — uses fresh `resolved.sequenceNumber`

### E2E Fix (staged, awaiting commit)
- **Root cause**: DB stores public keys as bare hex (`04abcd...`) but UI expects `0x04abcd...`
- **Fix**: Service strips `0x` before DB queries + E2E helper prepends `0x` to test account keys
